## Goal5842 two-generation internal technical completion (2026-09-03)

Exact V12 completed on RTX 2000 Ada and RTX A6000 Ampere at source commit
`04305fc820290cc183a599376f13d2fb48175233`. Each generation has 216 causal
receipts, 216 baseline subworker receipts, 108 composites, seven passing
stages, a complete hash-bound archive, and a Mac recount that is byte-identical
to the pod recount. The cross-generation gate passes for distinct generations
and UUIDs. Final authority seal: `5c8044d9...31e43`; focused evidence and
lifecycle tests pass 83/83.

Across both machines, generic admission is measurable but not the dominant
setup gap; target materialization plus native prepare accounts descriptively
for roughly 94%--97% of RTDL setup. Provider baselines remain adverse and are
retained, especially the public triangle route's avoidable per-ray host
materialization/reduction. Status is internal technical completion only;
external review, consensus, and public/manuscript performance wording remain
blocked. The next goal is a separately preregistered implementation repair,
not a rewrite of V12.

## Goal5486 LibRTS Exact Prepared-Phase Matrix

Goal5486 extends the Goal5485 prepared-index gate to all six exact official
archive member pairs on POD `157.157.221.29:25039` (RTX 4000 Ada, CUDA 12.8).
The verified archive identity remains size `23062425365` and MD5
`89e589f086038f1cd3af9e3ed67da8c8`. All six cases pass the same selected
geometry/query files to the pinned author binary and generic RTDL:

```text
case                             author count  prepared query wall
dtl_cnty                              136475          0.376076 s
USACensusBlockGroupBoundaries         148970          0.189807 s
USADetailedWaterBodies                118622          0.299463 s
parks_Europe                          109279          0.226963 s
lakes.bz2                             103189          0.178843 s
parks.bz2                             112729          0.408325 s
```

The batch summary is
`Paper-reproduction-apps/librts-paper/results/librts_goal5486_prepared_phase_batch.json`;
it records `case_count=6`, `matched_case_count=6`, and `matched=true`, with
per-case SHA-256 input identity and separate WKT load, index preparation,
prepared query wall, and native primitive fields. The largest inputs expose
the current front-door costs: WKT/MBR load is `404.471s` for `lakes.bz2` and
`553.019s` for `parks.bz2`; index preparation is `66.311s` and `85.547s`.

This is exact-input count agreement plus a prepared-query phase matrix. It is
not Figure-6 reproduction, pointwise relation equality, a performance ratio,
full-paper reproduction, or Embree evidence. Goal5486 is implemented and
review pending. The next engineering decision is whether to optimize the
app-owned WKT/MBR front door or first promote/measure a generic columnar input
API; any work must keep the phase fields separate and must not turn them into
an author-vs-RTDL ratio without an accepted denominator contract.

## Goal5487 Generic AABB Columnar Front Door

Goal5487 implements a generic host-column input contract:

```text
rt.Aabb2DColumns
rt.prepare_aabb_index_2d_columns(...)
```

The contract validates finite equal-length columns, bound ordering, integer
uint32 IDs, and native ABI size/offset compatibility. The OptiX packer creates
an aligned NumPy structured array and a ctypes `from_buffer` view while
retaining the NumPy owner. This removes Python per-row object/ctypes record
construction from the new path, but it is not device zero-copy: the existing
native prepare ABI still uploads host records to device memory.

Focused Goal5485/5486/5487 tests pass: 10 OK. Goal5487 is implemented and
review pending. The POD build/tiny gate passed: columnar and row-shaped OptiX
both returned `point_contains=2` and both reported `rt_core_accelerated=true`.
The next action is app-owned WKT/MBR column emission and a same-input phase
comparison; no device-zero-copy claim is authorized.

## Goal5473 LibRTS Exact Dataset Acquisition Decision

## Goal5470 LibRTS Generic Partitioned AABB Native Spike - No-Go

The authorized app-neutral OptiX prototype compiled and ran on local Linux / GTX
1070. Exact k=1 parity and k=4 layered execution were verified first: all 32
rows matched, total backward intersection invocations stayed 32, and maximum
work per backward ray fell from 8 to 2.

Four same-host five-repeat matrices then rebuilt the query GAS inside every
sample and compared `k=1/2/4/8`. All canonical rows and hashes matched. Peak
per-ray work fell substantially (`65->18` and `496->69` in the larger/dense
cases), but the best end-to-end movement was only `1.000x-1.009x`, below the
predeclared `1.02x` continuation gate. The temporary native/public API was
reverted. Four result JSON files and a no-go report preserve the evidence.

Goal5469 remains a public Python reference/planning contract. Native
partitioned traversal is not shipped and must not be reopened on the same
host-materialized canonical-row execution model.

## Goals5468-5469 LibRTS Ray-Multicast Feasibility And Generic Contract

The official paper Section 3.4 and pinned `RTSpatial@7c54c181` source were
mapped to the concrete mechanism: stable modulo primitive partitioning into
disjoint z layers, per-ray layer fanout through a two-dimensional launch,
payload layer filtering, and sampled power-of-two cost selection. Historical
query batching, prepared replay, multi-stream execution, and dynamic worklists
are explicitly not treated as equivalent.

RTDL now exposes a Python-reference-only app-neutral contract:

```text
partitioned_traversal_fanout_plan
estimate_partitioned_traversal_selectivity
select_partitioned_traversal_fanout
```

A Contact-Manifold-style broad-phase consumer proves exact Cartesian pair
coverage and reduces static maximum primitive load from 8 to 2 at `k=4`.
Focused validation is 6 tests OK on Windows and Linux; both hosts generate the
same audit JSON SHA-256 `5a062a04...f2baef`. The complete current LibRTS suite
is 66 tests OK with five local OptiX skips. No native backend or runtime
speedup exists yet. The kill gate passes only for one bounded generic OptiX
POD spike after strict review; pair mismatch, host-side measured traversal, no
same-POD win, or app identity in the ABI must stop the track.

## Goals5466-5467 LibRTS Representative PIP

The PIP line now has a Level-B public-source representative input: 64 simple
Block Group polygons (`30,635` vertices) and 100K points generated by pinned
author `GeneratePointQueries` with seed 0. Regeneration reproduces the committed
query SHA-256.

The first large run exposed a real semantic mismatch:

```text
unmodified author = 71,626 rows
standard RTDL PIP = 71,624 rows
relation symmetric difference = 6 rows
```

The difference comes from the pinned author's float32 `(0,0)` sentinel vertex
layout and CUDA fast-math PNPOLY, not from a generic RTDL requirement. Standard
RTDL semantics remain unchanged. The final app route composes generic RTDL
OptiX AABB candidate rows with an app-owned Numba CUDA compatibility refine.

```text
unmodified author count = 71,626
instrumented author count/rows = 71,626
RTDL app-compatible rows = 71,626
pair-row equality = true
canonical SHA-256 = 7d30e35b...26c7b
Windows focused tests = 16 OK
Linux focused tests = 16 OK
```

This is representative same-input relation evidence, not exact paper data,
Figure 12, Ray-Multicast, performance, or full reproduction. Goals5466-5467
are implemented / external review pending.

The exact AE PIP source chain was pinned and built with app-owned compatibility
wrappers. On the discriminating three-polygon/five-point fixture, author and
RTDL OptiX both report four polygon-refined hits while MBR-only filtering would
produce five. RTDL emits all four expected rows. Goals5464-5465 are implemented
/ external review pending.

## Goals5461-5463 Generic OptiX AABB Native Sparse Refit

Pure Update now uses `rtdl_optix_refit_prepared_aabb_index_2d_slots`, validates
stable slots/IDs, uploads only changed records, and executes OptiX
`BUILD_OPERATION_UPDATE` against an `ALLOW_UPDATE` GAS. Insert/Delete/Clear and
all CPU mutations retain snapshot rebuild.

```text
Linux refit/mutation/amendment tests = 15 OK
LibRTS same sequence = [2,1,0,1,0] matched
models = sparse refit, rebuild, rebuild, rebuild
4,096 boxes:  refit 1.44ms vs rebuild 18.15ms = 12.62x
65,536 boxes: refit 25.07ms vs rebuild 391.67ms = 15.63x
```

The earlier full-array refit was only 1.20-1.27x because it repacked/uploaded
all unchanged boxes. Goals5461-5462 received `approve_with_required_amendments`.
Goal5463 corrects stale call-for-review numbers and closes the rollback test gap
on Linux hardware: rollback restores old records/GAS after a post-update fault,
and rollback failure poisons the handle so every later query/refit fails closed.
The amendment was externally verified; Goals5461-5463 are closed and approved.

## Goals5457-5460 LibRTS Generic Mutation Milestone

The mutation audit found a real system gap: author RTSpatial performs native
incremental GAS/IAS mutation, while RTDL prepared AABB handles were immutable.
RTDL now exposes the app-neutral `MutableAabbIndex2D` contract with stable IDs,
CPU/OptiX backends, validation-before-prepare, and atomic snapshot rebuild.

Functional evidence:

```text
generic Linux OptiX revisions:
  initial [(900,10),(900,20)]
  update  [(900,10)]
  delete  []
  insert  [(900,30)]
  clear count 0

patched-author / RTDL same sequence counts = [2,1,0,1,0]
patched-author appended ID = 2
RTDL appended ID = 2
combined gate matched = true
```

The pinned author source has a one-line instance-update buffer bug: it
allocates `tempUpdateSizeInBytes` but passes `tempSizeInBytes` to OptiX. The
disclosed compatibility patch corrects that argument. The unpatched mutation
probe fails with `OPTIX_ERROR_INVALID_VALUE`; the patched public API passes.

Validation:

```text
Windows query+mutation suite = 31 OK, skipped=2 (no Windows OptiX)
Linux Goal5458-5459 runtime suite = 8 OK
Linux Goal5459 structured OptiX gate = matched
Linux Goal5460 patched-author probe = matched
Linux Goal5460 combined gate = matched
```

Goals5457-5460 are implemented / review pending. No native incremental RTDL
mutation, performance parity, paper figure, full reproduction, or Embree claim
is authorized.

### Implemented, Review Pending

- Goal5141: generic cell-MBR backend feasibility audit.

- Goal5143: local OptiX probe blocked by missing CUDA driver.
- Goal5144: POD OptiX backend-assisted gate matched after correcting SSH key.
- Goal5145: dimension-generic cell-MBR front door reference, including 3-D
  synthetic row-table oracle.
- Goal5146: native OptiX 3-D AABB point-membership row producer matched on POD.
  This is the first native 3-D broadphase brick for the cell-MBR track, not a
  complete native Goal5140 backend.
- Goal5147: backend-assisted 3-D cell-MBR front door matched locally and on
  POD OptiX. It connects native 3-D AABB broadphase rows to generic NumPy exact
  filtering and nearest-state/frontier lowering.
- Goal5148: bounded native OptiX 3-D cell-MBR nearest-frontier backend matched
  on POD. It adds `rtdl_optix_collect_cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_3d` and
  `cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_native_3d_optix_columns`, moving exact
  point-to-cell-MBR filtering and frontier kind classification into native
  traversal for 3-D. It remains bounded: not full 2-D/3-D Goal5140 backend and
  not an X-HD performance claim.
- Goal5149: generic nearest-witness continuation from cell-MBR frontier rows
  matched locally. It adds `nearest_witness_from_cell_mbr_frontier_numpy_columns`
  and includes a non-Hausdorff facility/service-radius consumer.
- Goal5150: bounded3D X-HD cell-MBR frontier route gate matched locally and on
  POD OptiX. It connects generic grid-cell MBRs, native/NumPy cell-MBR frontier
  rows, Goal5149 nearest continuation, and generic max-nearest reduction. It is
  still not the author fused RT-core algorithm or a performance claim.
- Goal5151: representative Stanford sample256 cell-MBR frontier route gate
  matched locally and on POD OptiX under the existing min-bound translation
  contract. It uses the same generic native frontier producer, but still
  evaluates all 65,536 source-target pairs per directed pass, so it is
  correctness evidence rather than a performance win.
- Goal5152: generic nearest-cell-MBR seed pruning matched locally and on POD
  OptiX. It keeps sample256 correctness while reducing candidate point-distance
  evaluations from 65,536 per direction to about 1,200 total seed+continuation
  evaluations per direction. It is work-reduction evidence, not yet a performance
  claim, because the seed still does tens of thousands of Python cell-MBR tests.
- Goal5153: vectorized the nearest-cell-MBR seed selection. The sample256 POD
  OptiX seeded route still matched author/exact and reported about 0.37s route /
  0.48s total in a single run. This is a route improvement, not a fair paper
  performance matrix.
- Goal5154: added a same-POD seeded performance matrix for sample256 and
  sample1024. It separates author `Running.AvgTime`, author process wall, RTDL
  route, RTDL total, load, and exact-reference phases, and intentionally reports
  no ratio. Both cases matched author HDResult and exact reference.
- Goal5155: added explicit production-style `validation_mode=author-only` and
  per-direction route subphase timings. The sample256/sample1024 POD production
  matrix skips exact-reference validation (reported as null), still matches
  author HDResult, and shows seed/frontier/nearest-continuation as the dominant
  route phases. It does not authorize a speedup/parity ratio.
- Goal5156: upgraded the production profile to per-repeat phase runs and median
  phase summaries. The sample1024 profile shows continuation and seed dominate
  native frontier rows.
- Goal5157: vectorized the generic frontier nearest continuation. It preserves
  seed and tie-break semantics, keeps app-neutral metadata, and reduces the
  sample1024 production route median from about 0.289s to about 0.170s against
  the Goal5156 matrix. Continuation is no longer the dominant route phase.
- Goal5158: vectorized the generic nearest-cell-MBR seed. It preserves the
  lower-MBR-distance, lower-cell-id, and lower-target-id tie-break layers, keeps
  app-neutral metadata, and reduces the sample1024 production route median from
  about 0.170s to about 0.114s against the Goal5157 matrix. The largest
  remaining measured phase is now native frontier row production, followed by
  the vectorized seed.
- Goal5159: added a row-table-only mode to the generic native 3-D cell-MBR
  frontier helper and used it in the X-HD route. It preserves backward
  compatibility by keeping split-frontier materialization as the default, and
  reduces the sample1024 production route median from about 0.114s to about
  0.108s against Goal5158. The modest size of the win shows the remaining
  frontier cost is mostly native row volume/production.
- Goal5160: added an active-row-only native emission option to the generic
  native 3-D cell-MBR frontier helper. The default remains
  `emit_pruned_rows=True`, but the X-HD streaming route sets
  `emit_pruned_rows=False` with `return_split_frontiers=False` because the
  downstream nearest-witness continuation ignores pruned diagnostic rows.
  On the same POD production matrix, sample1024 frontier rows collapse from
  189472/82544 to 2272/2185, and the production route median improves from
  about 0.108s to about 0.079s against Goal5159. This is an RTDL route
  improvement only, with no author parity or denominator-aligned speedup claim.
- Goal5161: added a generic Numba executor to
  `seed_nearest_witness_from_nearest_cell_mbr_numpy_columns`, preserving the
  existing NumPy fallback and tie-break semantics. On the same POD production
  matrix, sample1024 route median improves from about 0.079s to about 0.022s
  against Goal5160. The recent production-route line is now about 13.6x faster
  than the Goal5155 baseline route (~0.301s -> ~0.022s), still with no
  denominator-aligned author speedup or parity claim.
- Goal5162: extended the post-Goal5161 performance/profile matrix to the

  reports sample2048 route median about 0.059s, and shows nearest continuation
  is now the dominant measured route phase at the larger representative size.
  This is profile evidence only, not author parity or a denominator-aligned
  speedup claim.

  `nearest_witness_from_cell_mbr_frontier_numpy_columns`, preserving the NumPy
  fallback, active/pruned row semantics, seeded current bests, and lower-id
  tie-breaks. On the same sample2048 POD production matrix, route median

  This is a generic RTDL route improvement, not exact paper dataset
  reproduction or an author-performance ratio. The call-for-review file is
  `history/internal_docs/call_for_review_goal5167_grid_cell_mbr_reduceat_2026-07-08.md`.
- Goal5168: added a generic Numba `prange` executor path for
  `seed_nearest_witness_from_nearest_cell_mbr_numpy_columns`, with `auto`
  selecting `numba_parallel` when Numba is available and explicit `numpy` /
  serial `numba` modes preserved. The same full public res4 POD matrix still
  matched author HDResult with `validation_mode=author-only`; route median
  improves from about 0.052s to about 0.039s, total median from about 0.092s to
  about 0.079s, and combined seed time drops from about 20.0ms to about 6.4ms.
  This is a generic RTDL route improvement, not exact paper dataset reproduction

  `history/internal_docs/call_for_review_goal5168_parallel_nearest_cell_mbr_seed_2026-07-08.md`.
- Goal5169: added fail-closed inferred-capacity retry for streaming native
  3-D cell-MBR frontier rows. When `row_capacity` is inferred and
  `emit_pruned_rows=False`, the helper starts with a smaller capacity and
  doubles on native overflow up to the former full `query_count * cell_count`
  capacity; explicit capacities still fail closed without retry. The same full
  public res4 POD matrix still matched author HDResult; route median improves
  from about 0.039s to about 0.036s, total median from about 0.079s to about
  0.075s, and combined frontier time drops from about 13.8ms to about 10.2ms.
  This is a generic RTDL route improvement, not exact paper dataset reproduction
  or an author-performance ratio. The call-for-review file is
  `history/internal_docs/call_for_review_goal5169_streaming_frontier_capacity_retry_2026-07-08.md`.
- Goal5170: added a race-free grouped Numba parallel executor for
  `nearest_witness_from_cell_mbr_frontier_numpy_columns`. The executor groups
  active frontier rows by `query_row_id` and runs one parallel task per query
  group, preserving seeded current-best state and lower-item-id tie-breaks
  without concurrent writes to the same query. The same full public res4 POD
  route still matched author HDResult; same-POD serial-vs-parallel control
  reports route median about 0.0357s -> 0.0340s, total median about
  0.0777s -> 0.0746s, and combined nearest-continuation about 10.0ms -> 6.75ms.
  This is a modest generic RTDL route improvement, not exact paper dataset
  reproduction or an author-performance ratio. The call-for-review file is
  `history/internal_docs/call_for_review_goal5170_parallel_grouped_frontier_nearest_continuation_2026-07-08.md`.

## Current Truth

The system now has:

- a generic 3-D reference/oracle front door for cell-MBR nearest frontiers;
- a native OptiX 3-D AABB point-membership row producer.
- a backend-assisted 3-D cell-MBR route that uses native OptiX broadphase rows
  and generic NumPy exact filtering/frontier lowering.
- a bounded native OptiX 3-D cell-MBR route that performs exact distance
  filtering and nearest-state frontier classification inside native traversal.
- a generic continuation from those frontier rows to nearest-witness columns.
- bounded3D and representative sample256 X-HD route gates that use the native
  OptiX frontier producer and generic continuation/reduction to match same-input
  author HDResult.
- a generic nearest-cell-MBR seed that substantially reduces point-distance
  continuation work on sample256 while preserving correctness, now with
  vectorized cell-MBR selection rather than a nested Python query-by-cell loop.
- a native active-row-only frontier emission option that lets streaming
  consumers skip pruned diagnostic rows before atomic append/copy/sort, while
  preserving the default full-frontier behavior for other consumers.
- a generic Numba seed executor that removes the large NumPy seed matrix and
  expand/lexsort intermediates while preserving the same deterministic
  nearest-cell and nearest-point tie-breaks.

It still does not have the complete 2-D/3-D Goal5140 native ABI backend,
representative/full-scale X-HD integration, or X-HD performance reproduction.
The remaining gap is performance: Goal5154 shows the current seeded route is
still far from author `Running.AvgTime`. Goal5155 separated production route
timing from exact-reference validation, so the next performance work is no
longer "remove validation from total"; it is to reduce the actual route phases.
Goal5157 removes nearest continuation as the dominant phase. The next
Goal5158 removes seed as the dominant phase. Goal5159 shows avoiding unused
split-frontier materialization helps only modestly, so the next prioritization
is native frontier row volume/production first, vectorized seed second, and
continuation third on sample1024. Goal5160 then removes the pruned-row volume
from the native frontier path, reducing sample1024 route time to ~0.079s. The
frontier phase is no longer the main measured cost. Goal5161 then moves the
nearest-cell-MBR seed to a generic Numba executor, reducing sample1024 route
time to ~0.022s. The next target should be selected from a fresh median profile
at larger representative sizes or from remaining grid/frontier/continuation
overheads, while keeping all author-vs-RTDL ratio claims unauthorized.
Goal5162 provides that larger sample2048 profile and points back to the generic
nearest continuation as the next measured route target. Goal5163 moves that
continuation to a generic Numba executor and reduces sample2048 route time to
~0.025s. Goal5164 records a same-POD route lock point across
sample256/sample1024/sample2048. Goal5165 extends the current route to
sample4096 and records ~0.041s route time. Goal5166 extends the route to the
full public Stanford res4 pair and records ~0.059s route time. Goal5167 removes
the avoidable Python-loop cost from generic grid cell-MBR construction and
records ~0.052s route time on the same full public res4 pair. Goal5168
parallelizes the generic seed executor and records ~0.039s route time. The route
is now relatively balanced across native frontier rows, nearest continuation,
smaller seed, and smaller grid construction. Goal5169 removes avoidable
streaming frontier output over-allocation and records ~0.036s route time.
Goal5170 adds grouped parallel frontier nearest continuation and records
~0.034s route time. The route is now limited by several small phases rather than
one dominant Python loop. The next target should be selected by fresh full-res4
phase evidence rather than by stale pre-5170 bottlenecks.

Goal5171 adds an app-neutral native frontier row-order policy for the generic
3-D cell-MBR frontier producer. The default remains sorted+unique for
compatibility; the X-HD streaming route can explicitly request native emission
order with `--frontier-row-order native`, which uses
`rtdl_optix_collect_cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_3d_v2` and `sort_rows=False`.

Goal5171 full public res4 same-POD matrix:

```text
sorted control:
  matched = true
  RTDL production route median = 0.03375815600156784 s
  RTDL total median = 0.07492182403802872 s
  A->B frontier = 0.005131959915161133 s
  A->B nearest continuation = 0.003344528377056122 s
  B->A frontier = 0.006028778851032257 s
  B->A nearest continuation = 0.0034433528780937195 s

native unsorted:
  matched = true
  RTDL production route median = 0.03309101611375809 s
  RTDL total median = 0.07387318462133408 s
  A->B frontier = 0.003724612295627594 s
  A->B nearest continuation = 0.004194587469100952 s
  B->A frontier = 0.004374288022518158 s
  B->A nearest continuation = 0.004571780562400818 s
```

Interpretation: native-unsorted reduces the native frontier phase, but some of
that cost moves into continuation grouping. Net route improvement is small
(~2%), not a paper-performance or author-parity claim.

Goal5172 adds a native inline-nearest mode to the app-neutral generic 3-D
cell-MBR frontier collector. The new v3 native symbol
`rtdl_optix_collect_cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_3d_v3` computes nearest-witness
payload state for inline cell rows during native traversal and emits only
offload rows to the downstream continuation. The X-HD route exposes this through
`--frontier-inline-nearest`, but the system capability is generic cell-MBR
inline nearest, not an X-HD primitive.

Goal5172 full public res4 same-POD matrix:

```text
same-rebuild no-inline control:
  matched = true
  native symbol = rtdl_optix_collect_cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_3d_v2
  RTDL production route median = 0.03279334306716919 s
  RTDL total median = 0.07399309426546097 s
  continuation candidate distance evaluations = 612923 + 539093

inline-nearest:
  matched = true
  native symbol = rtdl_optix_collect_cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_3d_v3
  RTDL production route median = 0.029158174991607666 s
  RTDL total median = 0.06985066831111908 s
  continuation candidate distance evaluations = 7354 + 0
```

Interpretation: inline-nearest collapses almost all downstream continuation
candidate-distance evaluations and modestly improves route median by about
3.64ms versus the same-rebuild no-inline control. Some work moves into native
frontier traversal, so this is a real but bounded route win, not an
author-performance or full-paper claim.

Goal5173 adds an explicit author-directed route mode to the X-HD seeded route.
Goal5126 proved author `HDResult` is directed input1-to-input2, so the route can
now run only A->B when using `--direction-mode directed-a-to-b`; the old
two-direction `symmetric-diagnostic` mode remains available for diagnostics.

Goal5173 full public res4 same-POD matrix:

```text
author-directed inline-nearest:
  matched = true
  direction_mode = directed-a-to-b
  directed_b_to_a = null
  RTDL production route median = 0.01536349207162857 s
  RTDL total median = 0.0567256361246109 s
  author_abs_diff = 4.440050771492565e-09

sample256 exact-and-author smoke:
  matched = true
  rtdl_matches_exact_reference = true
  route median = 0.0032787024974823 s
```

Interpretation: Goal5173 is a functionality-alignment and route-time improvement
for the app's production mode. It removes an extra diagnostic direction that the
author contract does not require. It does not claim symmetric Hausdorff in
directed mode, author parity, or full paper reproduction.

Goal5174 records the current author-directed, native inline-nearest route across
the representative Stanford graphics scale ladder in one same-POD matrix:

```text
sample256:   matched = true, route median = 0.003070324659347534 s
sample1024:  matched = true, route median = 0.005820967257022858 s
sample2048:  matched = true, route median = 0.006353408098220825 s
sample4096:  matched = true, route median = 0.010627664625644684 s
res4full:    matched = true, route median = 0.014921210706233978 s
```

All cases use `direction_mode=directed-a-to-b`, leave `directed_b_to_a=null`,
and match author `HDResult` with abs diff under 7e-09. This is the current
Level B multiscale route profile, not exact paper dataset reproduction and not
a denominator-aligned author-performance ratio.

Goal5175 deepens X-HD exact-dataset provenance by extracting a structured
author-log workload manifest from the pinned author repository. The generated
artifact is:

```text
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_author_log_workload_manifest_goal5175_2026-07-08.json
```

It parses 281 current main-branch `expr/logs` JSON records, records 335 unique
author input paths, and confirms that zero logged input files are present in the
author repo/current machine. It also inventories 41755
`origin/paper:expr/for_the_paper` JSON blobs as inventory-only rather than
parsed workload records. This is stronger provenance than the earlier Table
matrix, but it still does not provide input bytes, input hashes, public source
snapshot hashes, or proof that a reconstructed dataset is byte-identical to the
paper inputs.

paths.

## Memory Consolidation After Goal5188

After Goal5188, the repository memory was reorganized so future Codex/Claude/
Gemini sessions can resume from files rather than chat history:

- `AGENTS.md` now explicitly includes `memory/architecture.md` in the startup
  reading list and names Goal5189 as the next X-HD system target;
- `memory/architecture.md` records the current RTDL/app ownership boundary, the
  X-HD route shape, the Goal5186-5188 evidence, and the current generic
  nearest-cell-MBR seed bottleneck;
- `memory/todo.md` was condensed to the current review packet, current best
  X-HD status, and the Goal5189 implementation target;
- `memory/roadmap.md` now starts with the Goal5188 full-public phase matrix and
  the immediate Goal5189 generic seed attack milestone.

This is project-state hygiene only. It does not change RTDL code, X-HD route
behavior, or any performance claim.

## Goal5189 Local-Grid Seed Route

Goal5189 implements an app-neutral local-grid-cell seed strategy:

```text
src/rtdsl/partner_continuations.py
  seed_nearest_witness_from_local_grid_cell_numpy_columns(...)
```

The helper produces a valid upper-bound seed from a nearby occupied grid cell.
It deliberately does **not** claim to select the nearest tight cell MBR. The
existing nearest-MBR seed remains available and is still the default route
choice unless `--initial-state local-grid-cell` is passed.

Same-POD full public Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B result:

```text
nearest-MBR control:
  matched=true
  route_wall ~= 8.31s
  seed ~= 5.15s
  frontier_rows = 2052249
  cell_mbr_tests = 2824560830

local-grid seed:
  matched=true
  route_wall ~= 5.98s
  seed ~= 0.90s
  frontier_rows = 7590188
  grid_cell_probes = 37335261
```

The route distance remains `0.12572988629271128`, matching the Goal5186 author
HDResult with abs diff about `2.38e-9`. This is a route-local system
improvement, not an author performance ratio or full paper reproduction claim.

Artifacts:

```text
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_full_public_all_source_nearest_mbr_control_goal5189_graphics_dragon_happy_buddha_2026-07-08.json
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_full_public_all_source_local_grid_seed_goal5189_graphics_dragon_happy_buddha_2026-07-08.json
history/internal_docs/goal5189_local_grid_seed_full_public_route_result_2026-07-08.md
history/internal_docs/call_for_review_goal5189_local_grid_seed_full_public_route_2026-07-08.md
```

## Goal5190 Grid Branch-Bound Seed

Goal5190 implements a second app-neutral seed strategy:

```text
src/rtdsl/partner_continuations.py
  seed_nearest_witness_from_grid_branch_bound_numpy_columns(...)
```

This strategy expands grid shells and scans cells while a grid-cell AABB lower
bound can still improve the current point witness. It is tighter than the
Goal5189 local-grid seed, but it is also much more expensive.

Same-POD full public Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B comparison:

```text
nearest-MBR control:
  matched=true
  route_wall ~= 8.31s
  seed ~= 5.15s
  frontier_rows = 2052249

local-grid seed:
  matched=true
  route_wall ~= 5.98s
  seed ~= 0.90s
  frontier_rows = 7590188

grid-branch-bound seed:
  matched=true
  route_wall ~= 7.71s
  seed ~= 4.60s
  frontier_rows = 1811625
```

Conclusion: branch-bound is a useful optional strategy/control, but it does not
replace local-grid as the current fastest full-public route strategy.

Artifacts:

## Goal5191 Inline-Nearest Threshold And Empty-Frontier Fast Path

Goal5191 measures larger generic native inline-nearest thresholds on the
full-public Stanford Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B route after Goal5189's
local-grid seed. It also adds a small route-runner fast path:

```text
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/scripts/run_xhd_cell_mbr_frontier_route_gate.py
  _nearest_from_complete_frontier_state(...)
```

The fast path is used only when the native frontier collector returns
`row_count == 0`; it converts the native `nearest_state` into generic nearest
columns and fails closed if source ids mismatch, any nearest item id is
negative, or any nearest distance is non-finite. It is app-route orchestration,
not a new RTDL core primitive.

Full-public Level-B threshold sweep:

```text
Goal5189 local-grid baseline:
  max_inline_points = 64
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 5.982s
  frontier_rows = 7,590,188
  nearest_continuation ~= 2.031s

Goal5191 inline128:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 4.970s
  frontier_rows = 3,647,552
  nearest_continuation ~= 1.147s

Goal5191 inline256:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 4.027s
  frontier_rows = 505,884
  nearest_continuation ~= 0.337s

Goal5191 inline512:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 3.723s
  frontier_rows = 0
  nearest_continuation ~= 0.155s

Goal5191 inline512 + empty-frontier fast path:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 3.648s
  total ~= 6.383s
  frontier_rows = 0
  nearest_executor = complete_frontier_state_passthrough
  nearest_continuation ~= 0.016s
  author_abs_diff ~= 2.38e-9
```

Current bottleneck after Goal5191:

```text
native frontier / inline-nearest collector ~= 2.00s
local-grid seed ~= 0.88s
```

Therefore Python continuation is no longer the correct target for this route.
Any next route work should target the generic native inline-nearest collector,
the local-grid seed, or pause route optimization for review/provenance work.

Artifacts:

```text
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_full_public_all_source_local_grid_seed_inline128_goal5191_graphics_dragon_happy_buddha_2026-07-08.json
```

## Goal5192 Native Inline-Nearest Telemetry

Goal5192 adds optional diagnostic counters to the generic native 3-D
cell-MBR inline-nearest collector. The previous visible count
`total_candidate_distance_evaluations` did not include point-distance work
performed inside the OptiX any-hit inline-nearest payload. Goal5192 adds a v4
native symbol and Python metadata for:

```text
inline_stats_collected
inline_cell_hit_count
inline_point_evaluation_count
```

POD validation:

```text
native symbol = rtdl_optix_collect_cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_3d_v4
focused POD tests = 16 OK
```

Full-public Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B no-telemetry control:

```text
matched = true
route_wall ~= 3.702s
frontier_rows = 0
nearest_continuation ~= 0.016s
native inline-nearest collector ~= 2.037s
```

Full-public Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B telemetry run:

```text
matched = true
route_wall ~= 3.873s
frontier_rows = 0
inline_cell_hit_count = 12,003,138
inline_point_evaluation_count = 1,242,677,739
visible seed/continuation candidate evaluations = 23,668,840
```

Interpretation: after Goal5191, Python continuation is not the bottleneck. The
remaining native collector floor is explained by about `1.24B` inline point
evaluations in native OptiX payload code. Telemetry uses atomic counters and is
diagnostic; it must not replace the no-telemetry control as the best performance
route.


## Goal5193 Grid-Cell-Budget Seed And Inline Threshold No-Go

Goal5193 tried two generic route attacks after Goal5192:

1. add `seed_nearest_witness_from_grid_cell_budget_numpy_columns`, a generic
   bounded nearby-grid-cell seed that scans at most N occupied grid cells per
   query;
2. fill in intermediate inline thresholds `320`, `384`, and `448` between the
   earlier `256` and `512` points.

Focused validation:

```text
local tests = 20 OK
POD tests = 20 OK
```

Full-public Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B route findings:

```text
local-grid inline512 no-telemetry control:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 3.702s
  seed ~= 0.899s
  native inline ~= 2.037s

grid-cell-budget=1 inline512:
  matched = true

  native inline ~= 2.253s
  inline_point_evaluation_count ~= 2.18B in telemetry

  inline_point_evaluation_count ~= 1.35B
  seed ~= 1.958s

grid-cell-budget=8 inline512 telemetry:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 8.142s
  inline_point_evaluation_count ~= 0.93B
  seed ~= 2.250s
```

Budgeted grid-cell seed is generic and correct, but it should not replace the
current local-grid seed for this route. Budget 1 is slightly slower than the
current no-telemetry control; larger budgets spend too much seed time.

The current default remains:

## Goal5194 Payload Current-Best Pruning

Goal5194 fixes a generic native inline-nearest traversal inefficiency: the
OptiX any-hit program updated the nearest-state payload after scanning an
inline cell, but later cell classification still used only the original query
seed distance. The native path now prunes later cells against the updated
payload current best distance.

Files changed:

```text
src/native/optix/rtdl_optix_workloads.cpp
src/rtdsl/partner_continuations.py
tests/goal5194_payload_current_best_pruning_test.py
```

Validation:

```text
local focused tests = 13 OK
POD build-optix = OK
POD focused tests = 13 OK
```

Full-public Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B route findings:

```text
Goal5192 no-telemetry control:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 3.702s
  frontier/native inline ~= 2.037s

Goal5194 warmed no-telemetry rerun1:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 3.465s
  frontier/native inline ~= 1.793s

Goal5194 warmed no-telemetry rerun2:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 3.447s
  frontier/native inline ~= 1.792s

Goal5194 telemetry:
  matched = true
  inline_cell_hit_count = 3,641,962
  inline_point_evaluation_count = 400,610,300
```

Compared with Goal5192 telemetry, inline cell hits drop from about `12.00M` to
`3.64M`, and inline point evaluations drop from about `1.24B` to `0.40B`.
Timing improves modestly and route-locally; this is not an author-vs-RTDL
performance ratio.

The current default route is now:

```text
local-grid-cell + max_inline_points=512
  + payload-current-best pruning
  + empty-frontier passthrough
```

## Goal5195 Intersection-Stage Current-Best Pruning

Goal5195 moves the Goal5194 dynamic nearest-state prune one stage earlier in
the generic native 3-D cell-MBR OptiX route. For inline-nearest collection with
no diagnostic pruned rows requested, the intersection program now decodes the
payload current best and returns before `optixReportIntersection` when a cell's
minimum distance is strictly greater than that current best. Equal-distance
cells are still reported, preserving lower-id tie-break opportunities.

Files changed:

```text
src/native/optix/rtdl_optix_workloads.cpp
src/rtdsl/optix_runtime.py
src/rtdsl/partner_continuations.py
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/scripts/run_xhd_cell_mbr_frontier_route_gate.py
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/scripts/run_xhd_full_public_subset_scaling_gate.py
tests/goal5195_intersection_current_best_pruning_test.py
```

```text

POD build-optix = OK
POD focused tests = 15 OK
```

Full-public Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B findings:

```text

  route_wall ~= 3.456s
  native frontier / inline ~= 1.792s

Goal5195 warmed no-telemetry run2:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 2.644s
  native frontier / inline ~= 0.960s

Goal5195 warmed no-telemetry run3:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 2.617s
  native frontier / inline ~= 0.928s

Goal5195 final metadata confirmation run:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 2.553s
  native frontier / inline ~= 0.932s
  inline_nearest_pruning = payload_current_best_min_cell_distance_gt_best
  intersection_pruning = payload_current_best_before_report_intersection
```

Telemetry remains:

```text
inline_cell_hit_count = 3,641,962
inline_point_evaluation_count = 400,610,300
```

Interpretation: Goal5195 reduces intersection/any-hit overhead for cells already
excluded by the updated payload current best. It does not further reduce point
evaluations inside accepted inline cells, so the telemetry counters match
Goal5194. The current default route is local-grid-cell + inline512 +
payload-current-best pruning + intersection-stage current-best pruning +
empty-frontier passthrough.

## Goal5196 Local-Grid Dense Lookup

Goal5196 changes the generic grid-cell seed lookup from repeated binary search
over `original_cell_ids` to a dense encoded-cell position table when the grid
volume is below `dense_lookup_max_cells`. Oversized grids fall back to the
previous binary-search lookup. The dense lookup covers local-grid,
grid-cell-budget, and grid-branch-bound seed helpers.

Files changed:

```text
src/rtdsl/partner_continuations.py
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/scripts/run_xhd_cell_mbr_frontier_route_gate.py

```

```text
POD extended seed tests = 17 OK
```

```text
  local-grid seed ~= 0.855s
  native frontier / inline ~= 0.932s

Goal5196 run1:

```

Interpretation: dense lookup cuts the seed phase by about 35% on this route and
moves total route wall to about `2.26s`. It does not change native frontier /
inline semantics or point-distance scanning. Dense budget and branch-bound
variants remain no-go controls for this workload; the default remains dense
local-grid.

## Goal5197 Intersection Attribute And Lazy Row Distance

Goal5197 changes the generic native 3-D cell-MBR OptiX route so the
intersection program reports `min_sq` to any-hit via two OptiX attributes. The
any-hit program reuses that value instead of recomputing query-to-cell minimum
distance, and it computes row-only min/max distances only when a row is emitted.

Goal5198 measures whether simple grid-shape tuning can reduce the remaining

POD:

```text

preflight = POD_OK

```

All successful runs use the current route family:

```text
initial_state = local-grid-cell
```

Interpretation: finer grids reduce point-distance evaluations but increase seed
and inline cell-hit overhead enough to make the route slower. 32^3 remains the
current default among tested shapes. This closes simple grid-shape tuning as a
no-go for this route and points the next real implementation at the generic
native inline-nearest execution model or a stronger generic spatial index.

## Goal5199 Trace-Tmax Bound No-Go

Goal5199 tested a generic native OptiX cell-MBR traversal hypothesis: replace
the current infinite ray `tmax` with a bound derived from radius or the initial
current-best distance plus epsilon. The aim was to reduce broadphase cell hits
before the custom intersection / any-hit programs run.

Temporary code changed `__raygen__cell_mbr_frontier3d`, exposed a
`trace_tmax_policy` metadata field, and added a focused source/adapter test.
That code was used only for the experiment and then reverted after the no-go.

Validation before the POD route:

```text

trace-tmax final2:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 2.320s
  seed ~= 0.599s
  native frontier / inline ~= 0.951s
  inline_cell_hit_count = 3,641,962
  inline_point_evaluation_count = 400,610,300
```

Interpretation: the trace-tmax bound does not reduce the hit set or the inline
point scan work versus Goal5198's 32^3 baseline. The warm route does not improve
over the current best (`~2.24-2.28s`). The change was reverted locally and on
the POD, and the POD native library was rebuilt back to mainline.

## Goal5200 Native CUDA Local-Grid Seed No-Go

Goal5200 tested whether the generic local-grid-cell nearest-state seed should
move from the current Numba CPU executor into a native CUDA executor. The goal
was to attack the remaining `initial_state_seed` floor on the full-public
Dragon/HappyBuddha Level-B route without adding X-HD-specific semantics.

Implementation:

```text
native symbol = rtdl_optix_seed_nearest_witness_local_grid_3d
Python wrapper = seed_nearest_witness_local_grid_cell_3d_cuda
partner executor flag = executor="native_cuda"
route CLI = --local-grid-seed-executor native_cuda
default executor = auto  # still selects the existing Numba path
```

The native helper is app-neutral: it consumes generic 3-D point columns,
encoded cell ids, dense cell-position lookup, point spans, grid shape, and
bounds. It returns nearest witness ids/distances for source rows. It does not
encode X-HD, Hausdorff, author, or paper semantics.

Validation:

```text
local focused tests = 12 OK
local py_compile = OK
POD preflight = POD_OK
POD build-optix = OK
POD focused tests = 20 OK
small native CUDA call matched the existing auto/Numba ids and distances
```

Same-POD full-public Dragon/HappyBuddha comparison:

```text
auto / Numba control:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 2.258s
  initial_state_seed ~= 0.563s
  initial_seed_executor = numba_parallel

native CUDA seed:
  matched = true
  route_wall ~= 2.436s
  initial_state_seed ~= 0.958s
  initial_seed_executor = native_cuda
```

Interpretation: the native CUDA seed is correct, but slower than the current
auto/Numba seed on the same POD and same full-public Level-B route. The likely
cause is that this implementation adds host-to-native marshaling and CUDA
launch/workspace overhead without making the surrounding target/grid state
device-resident. Therefore the default route must remain `auto` / Numba.

## Goal5202 Packed Coordinate Matrix Reuse

Goal5202 added a generic point-column packing convention:

```text
coordinate_matrix
coordinate_matrix_fields
```

and a helper:

```text
_point_matrix_for_fields(...)
```

The helper reuses a supplied packed coordinate matrix only when fields, shape,
and consistency with the coordinate columns are proven; stale matrices fail
closed. The current hot X-HD route uses this generic convention in two generic
front doors:

```text
seed_nearest_witness_from_local_grid_cell_numpy_columns
cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_native_3d_optix_columns
```

Validation:

```text

local adjacent X-HD/frontier tests = 25 OK
local py_compile = OK
```

## Goal5203 NumPy Matrix Input Loader

Goal5203 keeps the legacy X-HD row-based input helpers for bounded/reference
gates, but moves the high-volume full-public Level-B route to an app-owned
NumPy matrix input front door:

```text
load_ascii_ply_vertex_matrix
load_points_matrix
translate_point_matrix_to_min_bound
point_matrix_to_rows
```

## Goal5204 Linear Max-Nearest Reduction

Goal5204 changes the generic reducer:

```text
max_nearest_distance_witness_numpy_columns
```

from full-array `np.lexsort((item_ids, group_ids, -distances))` to finite
`max(distances)` plus lexsort over only exact maximum-distance ties. Tie
semantics are unchanged: maximum nearest distance, then smallest group/source
index, then smallest item id. Non-finite distances retain the old full-lexsort
fallback. The route summary now exposes `max_reduction_strategy` and
`max_tie_candidate_count`.

## Goal5205 Fast ASCII PLY Matrix Loader

Goal5205 changes the app-owned public PLY matrix loader:

```text
old: Python per-line split()/float() into a coordinate matrix
new: np.loadtxt(skiprows=header_lines, max_rows=vertex_count, usecols=coordinate_indices)
```

The legacy row loader remains available by converting the matrix output back to
tuple rows. The loader still accepts only ASCII PLY and fails closed on binary
PLY or malformed headers.

## Goal5208 Inline Threshold Lowering No-Go

Goal5208 tested lowering `max_inline_points` under the same Goal5207 explicit
warmup protocol:

```text
512 baseline
384
256
128
```

All variants matched the Goal5186 author HDResult, but lower thresholds did not
produce a meaningful improvement.

Measured case results:

```text
max_inline_points = 512:
  route_wall ~= 0.626s
  case_total ~= 0.809s
  frontier_rows = 0
  nearest_continuation ~= 0.0008s
  OptiX launch ~= 0.373s

max_inline_points = 384:
  route_wall ~= 0.623s
  case_total ~= 0.803s
  frontier_rows = 16,502
  nearest_continuation ~= 0.0166s
  OptiX launch ~= 0.370s
  full run including warmup is worse than 512

max_inline_points = 256:
  route_wall ~= 0.671s
  case_total ~= 0.848s
  frontier_rows = 146,548
  nearest_continuation ~= 0.042s
  OptiX launch ~= 0.360s

max_inline_points = 128:
  route_wall ~= 1.065s
  case_total ~= 1.244s
  frontier_rows = 1,286,627
  nearest_continuation ~= 0.404s
  OptiX launch ~= 0.317s
```

Interpretation:

```text
Lowering the threshold reduces native inline OptiX work only by materializing
frontier rows and pushing work into continuation. The tradeoff is unfavorable.
```

Current decision:

```text
Keep max_inline_points = 512.
Do not reopen lower-threshold tuning without a changed execution model or new evidence.
```

## Goal5211 Planned Global-Bound Early-Break Attack

Post-Goal5210, the next meaningful X-HD route attack is not another local
micro-tuning knob. The author X-HD OptiX path maintains global directed-HD
state:

```text
global cmax2 = best known Hausdorff lower/current max
per-query cmin2 = nearest distance found for the current source query
```

Author shaders can abort a query once a candidate distance is already
`<= cmax2`, because that source can no longer increase the directed Hausdorff
maximum. They also update the global bound from completed queries. The current
RTDL route computes each query nearest independently inside the generic
cell-MBR inline-nearest path, so it lacks this global max-nearest reduction
state during traversal.

Goal5211 should test an optional, generic, app-neutral global-bound early-break
contract for max-nearest / directed-Hausdorff reductions:

```text
native cell-MBR inline-nearest traversal reads a device global bound;
completed non-aborted queries atomically update that bound;
queries that find any distance <= current bound can abort early;
distance result remains valid for directed-HD max reduction;
per-source witness may be approximate for early-aborted rows.
```

This is a generic reduction/traversal optimization candidate, not an X-HD
primitive. It must be behind an explicit flag and must not become default
unless POD evidence shows it preserves the Goal5186 author HDResult and improves
the current Goal5207/5210 route regime. If it fails or only moves noise-level
milliseconds, record a no-go and keep the current default.

## Goal5211 Global-Bound Early-Break Result

Goal5211 is implemented / review pending and records a real route-local win.
It adds optional generic global-bound early break through:

```text
rtdl_optix_collect_cell_mbr_nearest_frontier_3d_v5
global_bound_early_break=True
global_bound_contract=generic_max_nearest_global_bound_early_break
```

The contract is valid for max-nearest / directed-Hausdorff reductions, not for
generic exact per-source nearest-witness consumers. When early break fires,
`per_source_witness_exact=False` because the early-aborted source witness may be
approximate, though the directed-HD distance is still gated against the author
HDResult.

## Goal5212 All-Source No-Copy Selection Result

Goal5212 is implemented / review pending. It removes unnecessary all-source
subset materialization in the full-public X-HD runner.

Previous behavior for `source_limits=all`:

```text
source_indices = select all indices
points_a_subset = points_a_full[index_array, :]
```

New behavior:

```text
if source_limit == source_count:
    points_a_subset = points_a_full
    source_subset_materialized = false
    source_subset_selection_contract = all_source_no_copy_view
```

```text
  route ~= 10.049s
```

Decision: keep `initial_state=local-grid-cell`. The heavier strategies are
dominated by seed construction time. This is a no-go for initial-state
retesting, not a negative result for Goal5211 global-bound early break.

## Goal5219 ModelNet40 Normalized OFF Contract Probe

Goal5219 is implemented / review pending. It follows up Goal5218 by inspecting
the author preprocessing contract and testing the same ModelNet40 pair with the
author `-normalize=true` flag.

Artifacts:

```text
history/internal_docs/goal5219_modelnet40_normalized_off_contract_probe_result_2026-07-09.md
history/internal_docs/call_for_review_goal5219_modelnet40_normalized_off_contract_probe_2026-07-09.md
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_goal5219_modelnet40_glass_box_author_normalized_probe_2026-07-09.json
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_goal5219_modelnet40_glass_box_rtdl_normalized_route_summary_2026-07-09.json
```

Author source/CLI contract:

```text
-normalize default = false
NormalizePoints(points):
  subtract per-axis lower bound for that input
  divide every coordinate by that input's largest axis extent
```

Author binary on official public raw OFF with `-normalize=true`:

```text
HDResult = 0.22594279050827026
Running.AvgTime = 4.292 ms
Input.Normalize = true
Input.Type = Float
Input.Translate = 0.0
input counts = [1107, 1200]
```

This exactly reproduces the paper-branch log HDResult for the selected pair and
also matches the logged normalized MBRs.

RTDL route:

```text
input_type = off
reference_preprocessing = ["normalize_each_input_to_author_unit_box"]
RTDL route directed_a_to_b = 0.22594284338858983
RTDL exact reference       = 0.22594284338858983
author normalized HDResult = 0.22594279050827026
RTDL exact abs diff        = 0.0
RTDL vs author abs diff    ~= 5.29e-08
tolerance                  = 1e-6
matched                    = true
```

Conclusion:

```text
For one ModelNet40 paper-branch pair, official public raw OFF plus the author
NormalizePoints transform reproduces the paper-branch author HDResult and
logged normalized MBRs. The RTDL generic route matches the same normalized
author run within float-author tolerance.
```

Boundaries:

```text
This is one-pair ModelNet40 reconstruction evidence only. It does not prove all
ModelNet40 pairs, exact paper dataset identity for every workload, author
performance parity, an author-vs-RTDL ratio, or full X-HD paper reproduction.
```


Nineteen categories/pairs passed all checks: author normalized HDResult equals

The one failing case is:

```text
range_hood_0124.off -> range_hood_0004.off
paper log HDResult       = 0.46497631072998047
author current XHD rerun = 0.466653436422348
RTDL normalized route    = 0.46497629417671404
```

For this case, RTDL matches the paper-log scalar while the current author XHD
rerun does not. The original paper-branch log reports `Algorithm=Hybrid`, while
the current author rerun reports `Algorithm=XHD`. A quick current-binary
`-variant clover` probe aborts with CUDA illegal memory access, so it is not
usable comparator evidence.

Boundary:

```text
ModelNet40 is now a strong normalized-public-OFF reconstruction candidate with
one known comparator/regime mismatch. It is not all-pair reproduction and not
full X-HD paper reproduction.
```

## Goal5229 ModelNet40 Author-Float32 Normalization

Goal5229 is implemented but not yet externally reviewed. It resolves the two
Goal5227 all-400 near-threshold failures without loosening the official
`1e-6` tolerance.

Root cause:

```text
author paper branch dispatches ModelNet40 through RunHausdorffDistanceImpl<float, 3>
author NormalizePoints therefore uses float coordinate semantics
RTDL app-owned OFF route had been normalizing in float64
```

Fix:

```text
normalize_point_matrix_to_author_float32_unit_box(...)
--author-float32-normalization
```

Full all-400 rerun:


## Goal5238 X-HD Author PLY Loader Translation Contract

Goal5238 is implemented but not yet externally reviewed. It audits the author
X-HD source to explain why Goal5237 only matches the author scaled-public
HDResult when RTDL applies `translate_each_input_to_min_bound`.

```text
main.cpp:
  --input-type ply -> InputType::kPLY

run_hausdorff_distance.cu:
  points_a = LoadPLY(...)
  points_b = LoadPLY(...)

loaders/ply_loader.h:
```

input contract, not arbitrary RTDL route normalization.

## Goal5243 X-HD Native Seed Phase Timing + Precompiled CUDA Kernel

Goal5243 is implemented but not externally reviewed.

Created:

```text
history/internal_docs/goal5243_xhd_native_seed_phase_timing_and_precompiled_kernel_result_2026-07-09.md
history/internal_docs/xhd_midterm_report_after_goal5243_2026-07-09.md
history/internal_docs/call_for_review_xhd_midterm_after_goal5243_2026-07-09.md
```

Goal5243 first instrumented the generic native CUDA local-grid seed phase, then
removed the runtime CUDA module compile/load cost by moving the seed kernel into
the precompiled CUDA helper path in `librtdl_optix.so`.

Before precompile, same Dragon -> scaled AsianDragon route:

```text
matched = true

route_wall = 2.8376277461647987s
seed_outer = 0.8517266660928726s
seed_native_total = 0.698966517s

```

After precompile, three same-POD repeats:

```text
matched = true
author_abs_diff = 2.3747470656587666e-09
median direction_total = 2.3074675127863884s
median route_wall = 2.3075230717658997s
median total_wall = 3.056991830468178s

median seed_native_total = 0.202945411s
```

```text

```

Denominator-explicit current comparison:

```text

RTDL route_wall / author process wall = 0.868x
RTDL total_wall / author process wall = 1.150x
RTDL direction_total / author internal Running.AvgTime = 27.64x slower
```

The `Makefile` now auto-detects GPU compute capability for `build-optix` and
sets `-arch=sm_89` on the current POD. This fixed a CUDA 12.8 / driver 550
runtime PTX JIT incompatibility for precompiled CUDA Runtime kernels.

Remaining dominant phases after Goal5243:

```text
frontier phase ~= 1.31s
frontier OptiX launch ~= 1.17s
grid_cell_mbr prep ~= 0.61s
local-grid seed outer ~= 0.35s
```

Claim boundary remains:

```text
single Dragon -> scaled AsianDragon Level-B public workload only
matches author rerun, not paper log
not exact paper byte-input identity
not Figure reproduction
not author internal AvgTime parity
not full X-HD reproduction
```

## Goal5244 X-HD Frontier Grid Shape / Grid Point Order

Goal5244 tested whether the best grid shape changed after Goal5243 removed the
runtime CUDA module compile/load cost from the generic native CUDA local-grid
seed path. It also added a generic optional grid-cell point ordering mode.

Grid sweep result on Dragon -> scaled AsianDragon:

```text
96x60x72   direction_total = 2.3156108260154724s
107x60x72  direction_total = 2.4444775945693254s
128x60x72  direction_total = 2.5677917823195457s
128x72x72  direction_total = 2.6038735965266824s
128x80x80  direction_total = 2.774916686117649s
128x80x96  direction_total = 3.0377826169133186s
160x80x96  direction_total = 3.256009257864207s
160x96x96  direction_total = 3.4368246719241142s
```

Conclusion:

```text
grid-shape tuning is a no-go for this workload
96x60x72 remains best among tested shapes
finer grids reduce inline evaluations but increase frontier OptiX launch time
```

The optional generic grid-cell point ordering mode:

```text
cell_point_order = point-id      -> cell_id_then_point_id
cell_point_order = input-stable  -> cell_id_then_input_order
```

POD comparison at `96x60x72`:

```text

point-id     direction_total = 2.3126574978232384s
matched = true for both
author_abs_diff = 2.3747470656587666e-09 for both
```

This is a valid small generic option, not a major performance result.

## Goal5245 X-HD Native Grid-Branch-Bound Seed

Goal5245 implemented a generic native CUDA executor for the exact
grid-branch-bound nearest-witness seed and an explicit route shortcut:

```text
--grid-branch-bound-seed-executor native_cuda
--skip-frontier-if-exact-seed
```

Capability result:

```text
Numba grid-branch-bound route      = 31.3390s
native CUDA + frontier             = 3.6386s
native CUDA + exact-seed skip       = 2.4508s
native CUDA + skip + input-stable   = 2.4748s
```

All runs matched the author rerun value:

```text
author_abs_diff = 2.3747470656587666e-09
matched = true
```

Performance conclusion:

```text
current best Goal5244 route ~= 2.3042s
native exact seed + skip    ~= 2.4508s
```

So the native exact branch-bound seed is a real app-neutral system capability,
but it is a performance no-go for the current Dragon -> scaled AsianDragon
route. The exact seed scans too much work:

```text
initial_candidate_distance_evaluations = 2,140,080,898
initial_grid_cell_probes = 6,170,907,111
initial_state_seed ~= 1.81s
```

It should remain behind explicit flags and should not replace the Goal5244 best
route.

## Goal5251 X-HD Global-Bound Publish Safety Fix

Goal5251 found and fixed a real correctness bug in the generic native OptiX
global-bound early-break route.

Pre-fix ModelNet40 batch40:

```text
matched_case_count = 39 / 40
failed case = chair_0162.off -> chair_0131.off
pre-fix abs diff = 0.0009493921217607892
```

The same chair case with `global_bound_early_break=false` matched:

```text
author_abs_diff = 2.8536356611041924e-10
per_source_witness_exact = true
```

Root cause:

```text
the native route published a global bound from queries that still had deferred
frontier rows, so an upper bound could be reused as if it were exact
```

Fix:

```text
src/native/optix/rtdl_optix_workloads.cpp
if (kind == 2) {
    optixSetPayload_6(2u);
}
```

After rebuild:

```text
chair + global-bound matched, abs diff = 2.8536356611041924e-10
batch40 fixed matched_case_count = 40 / 40
largest selected ModelNet40 pair fixed matched = true
```

Fixed batch40 performance:

```text
route_wall_sec sum    = 5.2887017503380775
route_wall_sec median = 0.09036052599549294
route_wall_sec max    = 0.7171685770153999
```

Critical caveat remains:

```text
per_source_witness_exact = false
```

Review packet:

```text
history/internal_docs/goal5251_global_bound_publish_safety_and_modelnet40_batch40_result_2026-07-09.md
history/internal_docs/call_for_review_goal5251_global_bound_publish_safety_and_batch40_2026-07-09.md
```

## Goal5274 X-HD hd_exec-Compatible Memory Accounting Integration

```text
Running.Repeats[0].Memory
RTDL.memory_accounting
```

## Goal5277 X-HD Memory Denominator Alignment Decision

Goal5277 is implemented but not externally reviewed.

It audits the author source for Figure 11 `WL` and `WL Heavy Peak` semantics:

```text
/tmp/xhd-goal5112/author/src/hd_impl/hausdorff_distance_rt.h

WL:
  wl_bytes = 2 * n_points_a * sizeof(uint32_t)
  in_queue + miss_queue

WL Heavy Peak:
  peak offloading_size * 2 * sizeof(uint32_t)
  offloading_point_ids_ + offloading_cell_ids_
```

It updates RTDL memory accounting so `WL` is no longer phrased as a generic
"frontier row capacity" field that could be mistaken for author `WL`.  The new
status is:

```text
estimated_rtdl_frontier_row_capacity_not_author_in_miss_queue
```

artifacts.

## Goal5287 X-HD Figure 9 Disposition

Goal5287 is implemented but not externally reviewed.

It consolidates Goals5284-5286 and closes the current Figure 9 line as
author-denominator-missing.

```text

```

Reason:

```text
The Figure-9-like plot script expects four auto-tune variants.
Current run_all/auto_tune logs provide only two of those variants.
The missing variants are not present on pinned main or hybrid branches.
The checked-in auto-tune.pdf is a rendered artifact, not a reproducible denominator.
Training sweeps are separate logs and are not promoted to Figure 9 without an externally reviewed mapping.
```

Allowed reopen conditions:

```text
Regenerate or recover the two missing run_all auto_tune variants for the plotted workloads.
Produce an externally reviewed mapping from logs/train sweeps to the checked-in auto-tune.pdf quantities.
Obtain external review accepting a narrower Figure 9 question with explicit denominator limits.
```

## Goal5302 X-HD Census/TIGER Source Resolution

Goal5302 is implemented but not externally reviewed.

It resolves the first concrete source/conversion plan for the X-HD
Census/TIGER-like geo path. This goal does not download full datasets, create
WKT artifacts, run POD, run author `hd_exec`, or run RTDL.

Matrix:

```text
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_goal5302_census_tiger_source_resolution_plan_2026-07-09.json
```

Author geo contract:

```text
run_fig5.sh geo pairs:
  dtl_cnty.wkt -> uszipcode.wkt
  USADetailedWaterBodies.wkt -> USACensusBlockGroupBoundaries.wkt
  lakes.bz2.wkt -> parks.bz2.wkt

input_type = wkt
n_dims = 2
normalize = false
```

Author WKT loader contract:

```text
POLYGON / MULTIPOLYGON: emits outer-ring vertices only.
LINESTRING / MULTILINESTRING: emits all line vertices.
POINT: emits one point.
Bad geometry aborts.
```

Official source probe:

```text
TIGER2023 COUNTY national ZIP: status 200
TIGER2023 ZCTA520 national ZIP: status 200
TIGER2023 BG state shards: status 200 for sample states
TIGER2023 AREAWATER county-FIPS shard: status 200 for sample county
TIGER2023 BG national ZIP: timed out; do not assume single-file BG entry
```

Decision:

```text
First executable Level-B geo candidate should be:
  dtl_cnty.wkt -> uszipcode.wkt

Candidate sources:
  official TIGER2023 COUNTY + ZCTA520;
  or name-matched ArcGIS services already tracked by RTDL RayJoin assets.

Next goal should define the conversion probe or bounded fixture, not run a
comparison yet.
```

Validation:

```text
py -m json.tool Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_goal5302_census_tiger_source_resolution_plan_2026-07-09.json
py -m unittest tests.goal5302_xhd_census_tiger_source_resolution_test
```

Important no-go:

```text
The initial Numba partner attempt failed on the current POD with
CUDA_ERROR_UNSUPPORTED_PTX_VERSION because Numba generated PTX 8.7 while the
driver/JIT path accepts PTX only up to 8.4. This is recorded as a POD
toolchain/environment no-go, not an algorithmic mismatch. Triton was available
and was used for the successful generic partner route.
```

## Goal5312 X-HD WaterBodies -> BlockGroups Full-Public RTDL Route

Goal5312 is implemented but not externally reviewed.

It adds an explicit fail-closed 2-D -> 3-D zero-z lift so 2-D WKT inputs can
use the existing generic 3-D cell-MBR route:

```text
--lift-2d-to-3d-zero-z
```

It also changes the X-HD app WKT matrix front door to stream into a NumPy matrix
in two passes rather than building one global Python tuple list before
conversion.

```text

py -m unittest \
  tests.goal5310_xhd_water_bg_full_public_wkt_candidate_test \
  tests.goal5311_xhd_water_bg_full_public_author_ingestion_test \
  tests.goal5312_xhd_water_bg_full_public_rtdl_summary_test \

Ran 15 tests OK
```

Key result:

```text
paper-log HDResult = 0.8964367508888245
paper-log num_points_cell = 8

Goal5311 default author rerun:
  HDResult = 0.8970130085945129
  NumPointsPerCell = 15

Goal5313 paper-config author rerun:
  HDResult = 0.8964367508888245
  NumPointsPerCell = 8

RTDL exact-witness float64 distance = 0.8964380566690101
same witness float32 distance      = 0.8964367508888245
```

Interpretation:

```text
The Goal5311/5312 WaterBodies-BG scalar mismatch is explained by an author
configuration mismatch. The paper-branch logs use n_points_cell=8; Goal5311
used the author binary default n_points_cell=15.

The RTDL witness is self-consistent in float64. Rounding that witness pair to
float32 gives exactly the author/paper-log value, so the remaining
1.305780185645311e-06 difference is a numeric-precision boundary for this
witness, not a large semantic mismatch.
```

Claim boundary:

```text
This still does not recover exact paper WKT files by hash, complete Figure 5,
or establish performance parity. It upgrades the WaterBodies-BG public
candidate from "author default mismatch unresolved" to "author paper-config
value reproduced; RTDL float64 witness aligns with author float32 value".
```

```text
geo_osm_lakes_parks_allnodes
```

Exact dataset rule:

```text
Level-C exact status requires author files/hashes, byte-identical regeneration,
or externally accepted deterministic public-source equivalence. Matching point
counts, MBRs, statistics, HDResult, author rerun, or path names is not enough.
```

Ranked next action:

```text
Goal5318 - WaterBodies/BG exact-provenance search before any new performance
work.
```

Reason:

```text
WaterBodies/BG is the closest current geo candidate: tiny point-count deltas,
paper-like MBRs, author paper-config scalar reproduction at n_points_cell=8,
and RTDL witness agreement within the declared numeric boundary. It still lacks
author WKT file/hash provenance or proof that the public ArcGIS service snapshot
is exact-equivalent.
```

Purpose:

```text
Refine Goal5347's broad "exact-witness vs fast-scalar" blocker by auditing the
actual hd_exec-compatible entrypoint route and the existing exact-witness versus
fast-scalar evidence.
```

Key finding:

```text
run_xhd_rtdl_hd_exec.py defaults auto + n_dims=3 + execution=gpu to
cell-mbr-exact-witness, not cell-mbr-fast-scalar.
```

Exact-witness entrypoint evidence:

```text
ModelNet40 all-400: 400/400 matched author rerun, route_label=cell-mbr-exact-witness,
per_source_witness_exact=true for all cases.

Stanford representatives (Dragon->HappyBuddha, Dragon->AsianDragon, Thai->Happy,
Thai->Asian): all route_label=cell-mbr-exact-witness and
per_source_witness_exact=true.
```

Fast-scalar evidence:

```text
Goal5212 Dragon->HappyBuddha fast scalar route matches the author rerun scalar,
but per_source_witness_exact=false and global_bound_early_break_count is
409376 / 437645 sources.
```

Interpretation:

```text
The exact-witness route is not a broad blocker for the hd_exec-compatible 3-D
GPU entrypoint on reviewed Level-B artifacts. The fast-scalar route remains an
exact scalar-value / performance route only; it must not be used as exact
per-source witness evidence.
```

Goal5351 is implemented / review pending.

```text

```

Purpose:

```text
Audit what the author X-HD CLI variant names actually mean in source, so the
RTDL app does not confuse "accepts the same option names" with "reproduces the
same algorithms or Figure 5 performance baselines."
```

Source verification:

```text
The audit script was run against a local checkout of pwrliang/X-HD pinned to
commit 7bf41c8442d059c94f4178355c6d5a10571d9658.

source_verification_status = source_expectations_matched
```

Author variant mapping:

```text
eb      -> HausdorffDistanceEarlyBreak
nn      -> HausdorffDistanceNearestNeighborSearch
clover  -> HausdorffDistanceClover
itk     -> HausdorffDistanceITK
rt      -> HausdorffDistanceRT / stats algorithm "XHD"
```

Additional source findings:

```text
compare-methods is parsed by src/main.cpp but has no implemented switch case
in src/run_hausdorff_distance.cu, so it is not treated as a supported paper
variant for RTDL parity.

RT-HDIST is a Figure-5 script/log external baseline label, not an hd_exec
variant implemented by run_hausdorff_distance.cu and not reproduced by RTDL.
```

Current RTDL status:

```text
The RTDL hd_exec-compatible runner accepts eb/nn/itk/clover/rt as directed
HDResult value requests.

Non-rt author algorithms and performance denominators are not reproduced.

The rt path has partial Level-B value-route evidence only; it is not a claim
of author RT-core algorithm identity or Figure-5 performance reproduction.
```

Claim boundary:

```text
full_xhd_paper_reproduction_claimed = false
figure5_reproduction_claimed = false
author_variant_algorithm_equivalence_claimed = false
author_variant_performance_parity_claimed = false
rtdl_accepts_all_author_variant_names_as_algorithm_equivalent = false
rt_hdist_reproduced = false
```

## Goal5354 - Generic Radius-Growth Schedule Semantics

Goal5354 is implemented / review pending.

Files:

```text
src/rtdsl/radius_schedule.py
src/rtdsl/__init__.py
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/scripts/build_xhd_goal5354_radius_growth_semantics.py
Paper-reproduction-apps/x-hd-paper/results/xhd_goal5354_radius_growth_semantics.json
tests/goal5354_radius_growth_schedule_test.py
history/internal_docs/goal5354_xhd_radius_growth_schedule_semantics_result_2026-07-09.md
history/internal_docs/call_for_review_goal5354_xhd_radius_growth_schedule_semantics_2026-07-09.md
```

Purpose:

```text
Extract the radius-growth update schedule behind the author RT `tune_radius`
family into an app-neutral RTDL helper, while keeping explicit `-tune_radius`
fail-closed in the X-HD app until route trace mapping is verified.
```

Captured pinned-source rule:

```text
adaptive:
  reduced_factor = (last_input_count - next_input_count) / last_input_count
  for expand_factor in [8,4,2,1]:
    if reduced_factor < 1 / expand_factor:
      radius += expand_factor * cell_diagonal
      break
double:
  radius *= 2
add:
  radius += cell_diagonal
radius = min(radius, hd_upper_bound)
```

Important edge case:

```text
The adaptive threshold is strict less-than. `reduced_factor == 1/8` falls to
`+4 * cell_diagonal`, not `+8 * cell_diagonal`.
```

New generic exports:

```text
RadiusGrowthStep
RADIUS_GROWTH_MODES
RADIUS_GROWTH_SCHEDULE_CONTRACT_VERSION
radius_growth_step
radius_growth_trace
```

The core exported names are app-neutral and contain no X-HD / author identity.
The tests include a non-X-HD retry-radius consumer.

Current X-HD mapping status:

```text
helper_semantics_available = true
route_uses_helper = false
run_xhd_rtdl_hd_exec_explicit_tune_radius_still_fail_closed = true
```

Validation:

```text
py Paper-reproduction-apps\x-hd-paper\scripts\build_xhd_goal5354_radius_growth_semantics.py --output Paper-reproduction-apps\x-hd-paper\results\xhd_goal5354_radius_growth_semantics.json
```

Purpose:

```text
Audit author X-HD lb / heavy-cell offload semantics and existing RTDL generic
heavy-offload assets before accepting any explicit -lb author option.
```

Author semantics pinned from source:

```text
FLAGS_lb -> config.lb -> processing_threshold

lb=0:
  processing_threshold becomes UINT32_MAX, effectively disabling heavy-cell
  offload.

lb=N:
  cells with point_count > N are offloaded.

offload rows:
  (in_queue index, cell id)

reported author fields:
  RTTime, CUDATime, OffloadingSize, WL, WL Heavy Peak
```

RTDL status:

```text
The generic RTDL threshold rule cell_point_count > max_inline_points is
shape-aligned with author cell_point_count > lb, and existing generic
heavy-offload row shape / native telemetry / author-shaped OffloadingSize
mapping exist.

But this is not explicit -lb support.  Figure 7 lb=0/lb=N evidence and Figure
11 byte denominators remain unaligned.
```

```text
only 2600727 rows versus the author trace's 27133990 rows. The mismatch is
semantic, not a row-remap or ABI bug.
```

```text
RTDL v7 = status rows emitted at the existing generic frontier emission point;
therefore explicit -lb remains fail-closed.
```

Authorized next is narrow:

```text

It may not add X-HD option names or paper figure semantics to RTDL core/native.
It must compare row count, hash/sample, status counts, and feedback count
against the Goal5387 author trace v2 oracle.
```
